ParlaMint-ES_2015-09-15-CD150915.u1	The meeting was resumed. We continue with the debate on the opinion of the General State Budgets Bill for 2016. First of all, the debate on section 25, Presidency. For the presentation of the section, I have the floor, on behalf of the Government, the Vice-President of the Government and Minister of the Presidency.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-09-15-CD150915.u2	With the coming, Mr. President. Ladies and gentlemen, I appear in this rostrum to set out the general outlines of the draft budget of the Ministry of the Presidency and its subordinate bodies for the year 2016; general outlines of a budget that is framed in the coherence that characterizes the public accounts that this Government has presented this year and the previous ones. We are consistent with our commitments to Europe, with the progress of Spain and with the efforts of all Spaniards throughout the crisis. We are consistent with the reformist project that we have promoted and with the results that the Spanish begin to perceive, because these budgets respond to the responsibility that has always been demonstrated in the tasks of government and the recovery that has finally managed to start Spain. Behind it lies the country on the verge of rescue in severe recession and with a continuous and growing loss of employment. Spain is now the fastest growing country among the major economies of the European Union, above 3%. Today Spain is the one that creates the most jobs, with a growth rate equal to that of its economy. Today Spain is one of the main exporting powers due to the observed improvement of our competitiveness. We have managed to change course, so we must strengthen our position, as these budgets do. These budgets record for the first time since 2006 a primary surplus, i.e. the balance without interest, which means lowering the debt burden with the assurance that bills are now being paid. Budgets that put the general government deficit below 3% for the first time since before the crisis, allowing Spain to exit the corrective part of the excessive deficit procedure to which we had been subject since 2009. And budgets which reflect a reduction in the non-financial expenditure ceiling of 4.4%, but which at the same time raise social expenditure to 53.5% of the total budget. These budgets are presented realistically in the forecasts and rigorously in the decisions, with only one main objective: to boost economic recovery. That is to say, to ensure the consolidation of growth and job creation, but also to compensate citizens for the efforts made both during the crisis and in fact to channel the recovery. This is about laying the foundations for growth so that we can continue to improve well-being, ensuring the stability that recovery needs. That and not another is why we are presenting these budgets. In the face of those in this rostrum who support the extension of the previous year's budgets, I would like to point out one point. These budgets enable measures to be taken to revive the economy and restore efforts to the citizens. When you raise in this rostrum the extension of the previous budgets, ask the citizens the following questions. Please tell them that with the 2015 budgets we could not take one of the decisions that these budgets have. For example, first, raise wages to public employees by 1% instead of keeping them frozen. Secondly, pay half of the extra payment due and complete the full refund of this contribution to officials after the 25% reinstatement approved by the Royal Decree-Law that we passed on Friday. Thirdly, to increase the replacement rate of public personnel to 100% in priority sectors, such as the provision and management of active employment policies or the external action of the State. Fourthly, to extend the funds for the assistance and integration of applicants for international protection, not only the initial 40 million of the July budget, but the amendment of the 200 million that all parliamentary groups agreed last week. Fifthly, almost 8% increase the financing of essential public health, education and social services provided by the Section 25, Presidency autonomous communities and local authorities. Sixth, to increase the available resources of the autonomous communities by almost €7.5 billion, which are added to the additional resources for saving interest resulting from the loans granted by the extraordinary financing mechanisms, almost €3 billion, in the same way that an increase of €542 million is provided to local entities. Eighth, without these budgets we would not be talking about an increase of 9.3% in spending on education policy, 7.3% on culture and 7.7% on social services and social promotion. Nine, without these budgets, resources for active employment policies would not increase by 10%, nor could a new special orientation programme for long-term unemployed be launched. Tenthly, without these budgets we could not adopt a 2.9% increase in pension policy or improve it for women who have been mothers; moreover, without this government, without the first budgets, we would still be talking about frozen pensions. You may consider these budgets unnecessary, but I believe that these ten reasons are sufficient to justify their approval and should be explained to the citizens. [[Applauds]] Ladies and gentlemen, these are therefore the broad lines that inspire the General Budgets of the State for the coming year, and this is evident in each of the policies and is reflected in each of the departments. It is up to me to present the Ministry of the Presidency. It is true that we have a limited weight in the overall budget - 0.5%, EUR 455 million - and that the functions are mainly administrative, yet this ministry has coordinated the reformist programme of the Government and has taken on the new challenges that reality presents us as the coordination of the main crisis situations that this country has experienced in this legislature. The department's budget for the coming year will be marked by the following priorities: firstly, by the impetus of the reform of public administrations as an integral objective and the incorporation of transparency as a basis for democratic regeneration and, from the funding we provide to other bodies, the importance of improving our security at the present time and for the future and, thus, the reinforcement required by the National Intelligence Centre. I'll try to make this the axis of my department accounts exhibit. That is why, first of all, I will start with the reform of public administrations. It has been, is and will be an enormous task and an integral approach from which we have been dealing since the beginning of the legislature and which was placed in October 2012 in 122 proposals contained in the report of the Commission for the Reform of Public Administrations. Since then, 175 measures have been implemented under the impetus and follow-up of the office for the implementation of the reform, which is based in our department, and are fully completed; that is to say, today I can say that 80% of that report has been completed in its entirety. The savings for public administrations for all measures amount to EUR 22,877 million; with good management we have been able to save almost the same amount that the State spends on unemployment benefits, I think it has been worth it. To this are added the more than 2.5 billion savings for citizens and businesses, in addition to the 5 billion revenues from sales of industrial holdings available to the State. I am therefore talking about a total of almost EUR 30 billion, EUR 29.6 billion, to manage better, to do better and to spend better. But the effect of the reform goes far beyond the figures to reach the culture of the public, so that good management ceases to be an objective and becomes an obligation, to which will undoubtedly contribute the rules on administrative regime and procedure that will soon be in the Official Gazette of the State and that will, among other things, represent a determined impulse to the electronic administration and a reinforcement to avoid the proliferation of merely instrumental administrative structures; a reduction effort that has led us to suppress 2,194 public entities -107 in the State, 754 in the Autonomous Communities and 1,436 in the local authorities - so that this effort to avoid useless structures will become a structural dynamic. A dynamic that has also been very present within the Ministry of the Presidency itself in order to rationalize our spending and face up to new tasks. The department's budget, regardless of transfers to other seconded agencies, is further reduced by almost 3%, leaving it below EUR 130 million, despite the fact that we have been taking on new functions and tasks, particularly, as I said, coordination in crisis management, which requires the joint action of several ministries, administrations and institutions of all kinds at national and international level. Such were the health alert for Ebola infection, the management following the accident of Germanwings' flight to the Alps or, at the moment, the humanitarian crisis generated by the displacement of refugees arriving in the European Union seeking the security and freedom that does not exist in their countries of origin. We have taken on more tasks and very complex with less expenditure and no more staff, so it is up to us to be more effective and more efficient. All budget items remain frozen for 2016, with the exception of changes in staff and current expenditure, mainly due to the centralization of contracts and services. The Ministry of the Presidency has been making use of this rationalization formula throughout the legislature. By providing data, in all public administrations, only centralised purchasing has saved more than EUR 500 million. In fact, the information coverage services contract itself presented by the EFE Agency is centrally financed in one department - ours - with 38 million euros and savings of around 5, compared to when it was dispersed by the different ministries. In this year 2016 we foresee new experiences of centralisation and new savings, which is why the current expenditure experiences a reduction of more than 7%. Centralized recruitment is established in telecommunications or cleaning services, and these resources are transferred to the Ministry of Finance so that they can be managed in a more advantageous and efficient manner. Current expenditure items such as the State mobile fleet are also reduced, and austerity efforts in the representation costs that have characterized this department and the entire Government are maintained. We maintain the same departure as in the last two years and I can tell you that in both the Presidency and the Vice-President and the Ministry, what was spent in previous years was spent on only one. I must tell you, however, that the increase in budgets in the field of ministry staff is also caused by a CORA measure, that is, we increase staff but centralize a service so far dispersed. The telegraphic cabinets - of which you use them like me - have been centralized in the Presidency of the Government, which so far served separately in different ministries and bodies. Today we have concentrated this service in the Ministry of the Presidency and will reduce the number of personnel required for its provision through relocation or retirement mechanisms. Beyond that, the rest of the games remain constant. Secondly, and after the measures relating to the reform of public administrations, another effort we have had to make is the implementation of the Law on Transparency and Access to Public Information and Good Governance. Until July this year, the portal has had more than 3,700,000 visits and requests for access to information have risen to 2,677, of which 93.3% have been resolved. The information available for active advertising has grown by 60% in just ten months, from half a million records to 800,000 of those currently available, and in particular the categories of contracts and grants, which provide for a greater number of information, have been further detailed. We are now proposing new ways of speeding up and facilitating citizens' requests for information, and this will be reflected in the rules of the Transparency Act, which we hope will be forwarded to the Council of State in the near future. We therefore gather the experience of the launch of the portal, which has been a very important effort on the part of all public employees and which is an example of management and capacity of our Administration, which has come to stay. Thirdly, I would like to explain to you the budgetary management of the agencies attached to the department. I am sorry not to follow an argumental thread, but, as you know, the Ministry of the Presidency has agencies that do not have much to do with each other, except for the good management they are trying to give citizens. [[Several Members: Very well!]] I'm gonna start with the National Intelligence Center. The honourable Member laughs. Among the National Intelligence Centre, the Centre for Sociological Research, National Heritage and the Centre for Political and Constitutional Studies is not that there are many similarities, except for the effort they make to manage themselves well, but that is what belongs to a ministry like mine. I am going to focus on the National Intelligence Centre, which has also on numerous occasions aroused interest in its resources and resources by the spokespersons who are in the Commission for the control of reserved expenditure. I would like to point out that in 2016 the transfers you will receive from the ministry will amount to 240 million euros, 7.7% more than in 2015. This was a commitment made by all spokespersons in the Committee on Reserved Expenditures, the need to increase the material resources available to the National Intelligence Centre and which meet these budgets. We are contributing more than EUR 17 million more to its operation at a time when our security needs an essential reinforcement in the face of truly unavoidable global challenges. We are therefore trying to improve their capacity and funding. I cannot go into detail because it is not the task of this House, but I am at your disposal in the relevant Commission to detail what this increase in its budget allocation corresponds to. The mission of the National Intelligence Centre is to provide the State, rather than the Government, with the elements of judgement necessary to support its strategic decisions. The world and the challenges presented by the future demand from the centre new ways of making intelligence and greater specialization, while posing the inescapable need to increase its resources and enhance its capabilities to deal with the risks that affect the security and interests of the Spaniards as effectively as possible. The reality has demonstrated in the most dramatic ways the increase in the level of danger achieved by some of the global threats to our security. I am referring to issues such as international terrorism, especially jihadist terrorism, along with cyberterrorism and cybersecurity or new forms of intelligence with a markedly economic component. They will agree with me that any democratic country cannot stand behind and must have the best tools to meet these challenges. That is why we must also strengthen the capabilities of the National Intelligence Centre. This is also why, in collaboration with the Centre, we have adopted a new programme of action, a new strategic concept, a programming that includes tasks and resources, i.e. both priority areas and material, human and precise means to define a modern and competitive service. For this reason, part of the budget increase of €7 million will be directly linked to the increase in human resources, an increase that has also begun to materialize by authorizing the National Intelligence Centre to receive 125 people in what constitutes the first phase of a global medium-term endowment project foreseen in the new strategic concept of the centre. They are essential to provide the necessary coverage to the areas of activity that I have told you and that need to be enhanced. In this way, the availability of greater economic, technological, material and human means, together with the experience and effectiveness that the CNI has accredited, constitute the fundamental pieces on which to base an intelligence service at the service of a democracy and better prepared to respond to the present and future challenges, a National Intelligence Centre that contributes more and more effectively to the knowledge of those scenarios in which intelligence activity represents an added value for the development of State action. They will agree with me that this security and the protection of freedom are a matter of State, since the State, as I said at the beginning, is served by the National Intelligence Centre. The free societies that we respect and protect fundamental rights need to be safe societies because, ultimately, freedom and security are the fundamental principles of democracy and together we have a duty to preserve them. For our part, we have done so, trying to emphasize this endowment as one of the main tasks of the ministry. Transfers from other agencies attached to the Ministry of the Presidency, the Centre for Political and Constitutional Studies, the Centre for Sociological Research, National Heritage and the State Agency Official State Gazette, however, have been reduced, but this has not meant a linear decrease in their budgets. In line with the reform of public administrations, what we have done in this legislature is to try to improve the management of their resources. For example, various services and self-financing capacities have been integrated into centralized recruitment, because they provide good services that citizens recognize. For example, the self-financing of National Heritage has doubled, from 18% in 2011 to 34% in 2015. In the CIS, the Centre for Sociological Research has grown in similar terms, from 18.6% to 35.2%, and in the Centre for Political and Constitutional Studies it has grown seven points. All this has allowed us to reduce the amount of transfers that are provided from public revenues without diminishing the activities or benefits of each agency. In fact, some of them have launched ambitious projects. I begin with the only state agency attached to the ministry, which is the State Official Gazette. The self-financing capacity of the State Official Gazette is full and also generates a surplus that is entered in the public treasury, more than 31 million euros. We can therefore say that the State Official Gazette has been and continues to be well managed. Its budget for the next financial year maintains the previous one and amounts to 62 million euros. Moreover, it must be borne in mind that it has changed its tasks in recent times. The BOE has become a reference printing company for the editing and printing of both the editorial programme of the General Administration of the State and other works of its departments, which has increased its turnover by 50% and its customers by 20%. This has enabled him to develop new projects at the service of the citizen, for example, to give social utility. The BOE database already offers more than 5,000 consolidated standards that are updated within less than one day. I recommend it in the management of consolidated legislation when preparing its appearances, the State Official Gazette offers it immediately and free of charge. The A la carte State Official Gazette project already has more than 146,000 subscribers. I also invite you to use it, simply by joining that page you will have the notifications that interest you. The new collection of electronic codes has ninety titles and the number of downloads exceeds 3 million - good management - but now we have two major challenges at the service of citizens: first, the single edict table of public administrations. Until 31 July, 20,000 users have been discharged and 70,000 notifications have been published. On 1 November, the notary and registrar groups are scheduled to be incorporated into the use of the board. What is it, ladies and gentlemen? To avoid the pilgrimage of citizens of edictal board in edictal board when they have to know some administrative decision that affects them. The second important measure is also the electronic auction portal. All State-level auctions - judicial, administrative and notarial - will be combined at one point. They will be carried out electronically, which will allow for the replacement of face-to-face auctions, with the resulting savings, the improvement of accessibility and, of course, of neutrality. In just one month, on October 15, 2015, it will be operational for notarial auctions. Finally, we have launched a legal portal and a portal of historical law. The State Official Gazette therefore continues to make progress as a quality public service. National Heritage. It has also been able to increase its income, especially through collaborations for the holding of workshop schools, which are also a mechanism for job creation, and by improving ticket collection. Only in the first six months of this year have the revenues from visits of the real sites increased by 15% over the same period of 2014. The budget for 2016 amounts to more than 126 million euros, which represents a slight increase, which will be destined primarily for the necessary endowments for the launch of the Museum of the Royal Collections. The works of the museum, which have involved a total investment of 140 million euros, will most likely end in November of this year, and from there will have to start the works of museumography, equipment and restoration of works of art. The Centre for Sociological Research has also raised its expenditure to EUR 6.36 million for its self-financing. However, there is an increase in the number of goods and services in Chapter 2, from which surveys are financed. The number of studies grows, because I remind you - this I imagine will interest you more - that in 2016 the center has to complement two electoral studies, Galicia and Basque Country, in addition to the corresponding surveys by the National Statistical Plan and those that are part of their international projects. In addition, the dissemination of its results has improved through the ARCE project, which allows for the new Public Sector Information Re-Use Act adopted this year. Finally, the Centre for Political and Constitutional Studies has reduced its budget, but maintains its study and training activity, in particular its university master's degree in Constitutional Law. In short, ladies and gentlemen, these budgets reflect a serious and rigorous management of resources, with a firm and clear determination to promote new projects to provide better services or, more importantly, to ensure our rights. They are accounts adapted to recovery, prepared for reality, to offer and enhance those services that the ministry provides security to our country and that can help generate confidence and stability. This is what these budgets mean, those of the ministry I have just presented to you, those of its various sections, but also those of the whole of a project that constitutes an integral programme in which there is no point in going back. We must continue to move forward as a prosperous society, because it has not finished, and I believe that these budgets are a decisive step that must guide us in this recovery. Thank you very much, ladies and gentlemen, for your attention. [[Applauds]]
ParlaMint-ES_2015-09-15-CD150915.u3	Thank you very much, Madam Vice-President. In defence of amendments, first of all, by the Group of the Progressive Union and Democracy, Mrs Díez has the floor.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-09-15-CD150915.u4	Thank you, President. It is for me, on behalf of my group, to present the amendments to this whole section, which, as the Vice-President is sure to know, has much to do with the substance of the matter, with the substance of the policy and with the reforms that Spain needs in order to deal with the problems that our society has. It may be said to me - sometimes it has occurred - that the issues that our parliamentary group has tabled in amendments to this debate are not specific to a budget debate, but allow me to say that, since the dynamics are that the Group of the European People's Party does not accept any of the amendments tabled by the opposition, this intervention serves to reflect on the substance of the problems facing our society. Today is the day after the failure of negotiations at European level regarding the refugee crisis. Let us say that all the ministers who sat yesterday on behalf of their Governments had the best of will to deal with the crisis. What failed? The European structure has failed. Europe is not a state today, it has no capacity to deal with the problems of the European Community with supranational decisions. The nationalism of the Member States prevents agreements that require a European response from being reached. It is the structure of the country that, despite - let us put it - the very good will of all those present, made impossible a political agreement that was necessary to respond to a political crisis as well. Let's apply that at the national level. That is what my group wants to put forward in these brief minutes of reflection. Yes, we can amend the budgets, we have done so to the sections in particular and with partial amendments. We believe, for example, that not so many advisers can be retained while the staff rate is not being replenished. We believe that there can be no continuation of a departure to maintain the former presidents' lifetime pensions and that they are compatible with other wages at the same time. Yes, they are partial amendments which will, as always, be rejected by you. But the main question is not that, because although the partial amendments were admitted to us, Spain has a problem of structure, of capacity to respond to the real problems of the country, which have to do with the growing problems of inequality in Spain, which are not solved if the structural amendments are not faced, that is, if the Constitution itself is not reformed and the privileges established in some Spanish territories are not liquidated, if the fiscal privileges, for example, of some of the autonomous communities, the so-called special ones, which are not of common regime, are not reformed. The problems of inequality caused by communities that do not have a common regime but a special regime are not solved by extending this privileged regime to other communities in Spain, as the Minister of Foreign Affairs apparently wants in the case of Catalonia. That's not how the problems are solved. Madam Vice-President, you know - that is why I say that it is not a case of goodwill, of good or bad faith when making budgets - that, according to official data, in Spain, the non-financial resources per inhabitant in the autonomous communities of common system are on average 2,763 euros, while the average in the communities of foral regime is 4,829 euros per inhabitant. You will say: What does that have to do with budgets? It is that the budgets have to serve to solve the problems of Spain, and one of the main problems of Spain is inequality; that is one of the fundamental objectives of any government and therefore of the most important economic law of the year, which are the budgets. If the problems of inequality are not solved, the problems of justice are not solved, the problems of the structure of the State are not solved and, therefore, it is a useless law, beyond that it is a bad law by its structure, beyond that it is a bad law by how you have done it and beyond that it is a law that has no more objective - from our humble point of view - than electoral. If we do not solve the fundamental problems of an untenable and unsustainable State - politically untenable and economically unsustainable - we will be unable to solve the fundamental problems that our country has which, as I say, have primarily to do with equality. I repeat the official figure: the average of the non-financial resources per inhabitant in the autonomous communities of common system is 2,700 euros and the average in the communities of external regime is 4,800 euros. This is not solved with a different budget; it is solved by addressing the problem of substance and depth, addressing the structure of the State and ending the privileges established by law. That requires changing the system. As in Europe, the problems arising from the Commission's lack of structure and political capacity are not solved by meetings of the Ministers of the Interior and Justice. In Spain, they are not solved by a budget which, at best - and not in the case of the case - is nothing more than a sluggish one. Our parliamentary group once again takes advantage of this budgetary procedure in order to get to the bottom of the matter, in what Spain needs: not only partial amendments, but in-depth structural reforms. It needs a reform of the Constitution to ensure that the union of Spain recognized in it is a necessary condition and, therefore, something must be done to promote it and to guarantee a fundamental right such as the right to equality; that it abolishes the fiscal privileges of the Basque Country and of Navarre precisely so that there are resources for all Spanish citizens to live in the part of Spain in which they live, to have guaranteed on an equal basis the provision of the fundamental services and those rights that have been considered essential; and also that, once and for all, it structures a system of public function that has to do with modern Spain of the twenty-first century and we do not continue with the situation that we have been enduring so far. That is why, ladies and gentlemen, Madam Vice-President, Members of the People's Parliamentary Group, our parliamentary group enters the budget debate, understanding that it is the most important law of every year, to raise the reforms that Spain and the Spanish need; not mere patches or caps, but to go into depth with the fundamental problems that Spain has. This is called reforming the structure of the State so that Spain is more than the sum of seventeen autonomous communities and two autonomous cities and that the Government is more than the coordinator of the autonomous communities and the autonomous cities.

ParlaMint-ES_2015-09-15-CD150915.u9	Thank you very much, Mr. Xuclà. The Socialist Group, Mr De la Rocha, has the floor to table its amendments.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-09-15-CD150915.u10	Mr. President, thank you very much. Madam Vice-President of the Government, you have begun your speech by making an electoral panegyric, a summary of your electoral programme and a certain autoloa of your management. Perhaps in the first part you have intended to replace, supplement or remedy many of Mr Montoro's overall interventions on the budget. But as much as you do panegyrics, Spanish citizens do not forget their policies of cuts, their policies of inequality, and will not forget them especially when the December elections arrive. I was surprised by some of the things you said about this subject, for example, when you spoke of unemployment, obvious that EUR 10 billion has been cut for the unemployed. It is a budget against the unemployed, against the people who are unemployed: 5 billion last year and 5 billion this year. He talked about active employment policies when we are still at EUR 3 billion of the money that the Socialist Government spent on active employment policies in 2011. Let's go to section 25, Ministry of the Presidency, which you run. A section of small quantitative importance, which has rather an ideological sign in some of its policies and a horizontal character. In the debate in the Commission, both my colleague Nacho Sánchez Amor and I raised some issues in relation to the Transparency Act and the CORA. I do not want to insist on this, but I do want to refer to two aspects that you have just mentioned. You are satisfied that you have suppressed 2,194 public entities. Today, it has hidden another aspect from which they are also ugnant, which has resulted in savings of EUR 14 billion for public employment measures. This means, simply, that they have dismissed or not renewed interim contracts, extinguished - according to their own data - of about 400,000 public employees, which has resulted - I do not know if intentionally - in a manifest deterioration of public services and in a process of privatization of the care and service activity in our country. I am also surprised that you are talking about the impetus for public administration reform through the two laws reforming the administrative procedure and the legal regime of the public sector. Probably in the history of the Council of State there has never been a report as hard against these two bills as the one issued this time by the Council of State. The aim was to recover the memory of the victims of Francoism thirty years after the adoption of the Constitution. His recognition and dignity was and is a moral duty in political life and a sign of the strength of democracy. History cannot be built on the forgetfulness of the victims. We know that there are tens of thousands - 150,000 are estimated - of the disappeared who are in mass graves and in the cunetas simply to defend legality. They belonged to many organizations and, I must also say, to the Socialist Party and the UGT. His relatives do not know his whereabouts and his remains cannot be exhumed. Under the Historical Memory Act, the Ministry of Justice promoted the creation of a grave map; 2,382 graves were located, mass graves, referring to some 45,000 victims. Some 300 have been opened, rescuing some 6,500 bodies, but many tens of thousands of victims remain, bodies untraceable, unidentified, without a decent burial on the part of their relatives. The task is not that it is unfinished, it is interrupted because you have denied that there is a budget for these purposes. From 2007 to 2011, the Socialist Government allocated 26 million for the implementation of the Historical Memory Act. You, in 2012, allocated 2.5 million and from then on, zero. The reasons you have always argued are that there was no budget; cuts. They have claimed it; it is in the 'Diary of Sessions'. For everyone, less for historical memory; for the victims of Francoism there is still no euro. Therefore, the reasons are not economic, they are purely ideological, their ideological commitments to the past, moreover surprising; for the victims of Francoism, nothing at all. The overall funding for this programme is EUR 500 million, will be produced in two years and the estimated real beneficiaries that can pass through this programme is 1.264,000 people. ParlaMint-ES_2015-09-15-CD150915.u41	Madam President, ladies and gentlemen, this morning I hold this rostrum of the Congress of Deputies to defend and explain the basic lines of section 15, corresponding to the Ministry of Finance and Public Administration, and section 36. It is obviously not a question, as always in these presentations, of entering into the detailed presentation of the figures, which are already sufficiently reflected in the documentation accompanying the draft Law on General State Budgets, but, as is usual in these presentations, of explaining why these figures, why the Ministry's draft Sections 15, Finance and Public Administrations, and 36, systems of financing of territorial entities of Finance and Public Administrations, and the meaning of what represents the contribution of the ministry throughout the Government of Spain. We are at the end of this legislature, which has been a demanding legislature, in which we have had to treat an economic crisis with great social depth so that we can face the economic recovery that we are experiencing at this end of the legislature, in particular in 2015, and that the 2016 budget projects and guarantees for 2016 and beyond. This is the most important thing that the draft General State Budgets do and the most important thing that rests on the task of the Ministry of Finance and Public Administration. This ministry coordinates the budgetary action of the entire government, but in the state of autonomy of Spain it does much more. The Ministry of Finance and Public Administration also coordinates the budgetary policies of the territorial administrations, autonomous communities and local corporations, and on the ministry lies the responsibility for the fulfilment of the public deficit objectives for all the administrations of Spain. We are the interlocutor of the budgets of all public administrations with the European institutions and, therefore, the guarantor of the fulfilment of the deficit and public debt objectives that our country, as a member of the European Union and as a member of the euro, is committed to. The 2016 budgets again emphasize the importance of reducing the public deficit and public debt in our country, confirm the reduction of the public debt on our country's gross domestic product and make it compatible with a slight increase in public expenditure, which continues to be marked by rigour and austerity but increases, and does so because it increases the collection of taxes resulting from the new economic situation that our country is experiencing, in particular that which begins clearly in 2014, is deepened in this year 2015 and is projected towards 2016. With the new 2016 budgets, officials are guaranteed that their salary will rise by 1% after five years of freezing and with the drop in their salaries in 2010. This is the first time that officials have guaranteed this increase, this advance in the recovery of their economic power, their purchasing power, and they have it for all of Spain, since we are amending the basic regulation so that this increase can be transferred by all public administrations. Officials are also guaranteed the full recovery of the payment that was withdrawn from them in December 2012; the remaining 50% are guaranteed in these 2016 budgets. I am sure that, regardless of what happens in the general elections, officials will be charged the pay in 2016. You may change the composition of this House, but I can't imagine anyone at the beginning of 2016 going up here to say they won't charge half the outstanding pay. Therefore, they are budgets that give a lot of confidence and security to those who have also been the creators of this economic recovery through their professionalism and effort, which are the officials, who are making public services available to the Spaniards; therefore, with their capacity for action they are making possible economic recovery and job creation in all areas of economic activity, including the very scope of the Public Service, because the rate of replenishment of the Public Service in the new budgets is also projected to one hundred percent for much broader public services. We are therefore ensuring the provision of these public services through the creation of new jobs there too. In addition, the 2016 budgets are a guarantee for our country's pensioners, who see how they increase their pensions when we close 2015 with clearly negative CPIs in terms of evolution but compatible with the recovery of our economy. The 2016 budgets support tax cuts, e.g. corporate tax. The company tax will double in 2016 and its overall rate will remain at 25 %, which will make the new company tax when the capitalisation funds provided for in the new tax figure can reach effective rates of 20 % and even below 20 % for small and medium-sized enterprises. It is important that we note that there are already tax cuts programmed in the 2016 budgets, reductions that are key to the continuity of the economic recovery, to the deleveraging of our business sector and to attract new investments to Spain, as is the reduction of the company tax that I want to put in value this morning. Corporate tax has already been lowered in this year 2015 and this reduction will continue in 2016. And there's more. Tax collection continues to be strengthened in our country in 2015, as the Tax Agency is recording - and I will now give the latest figures in this regard - at a rate that shows that there is no economic slowdown in Spain, unless there is an economic slowdown that is compatible with the increase in tax collection, but we have not yet discovered the economic foundations that can support such an approach. In other words, tax collection is absorbing by far the fall in taxes, which only in the IRPF in the period from January to August stands at 2.6 billion euros, plus the decrease in corporate tax, thus reaching 3.6 billion euros, and I remind you that 25 % of the outstanding payment of civil servants has also been paid at the beginning of the year, and yet the growth of the agency's tax revenue in the period from January to August amounts to 5.2 % in homogeneous terms. It is a rise, in relation to the one that already recorded the period January-July, that comes to show that economic activity in Spain is recovering. And this rise comes from the expansion of tax bases for all the players in our economic activity: companies, the collection of VAT, professionals, who are also increasing to 16% the fractional payment of their personal income tax in the first half of this year, just as VAT payments for the same period I am quoting are growing to 11.2% for small and medium-sized enterprises and for the rest of the companies - large companies - to 8%. Therefore, we are facing a very important expansion of tax bases in our country, which makes the tax reduction already practised by the Government in 2015 compatible. I insist: we have absorbed a decrease of EUR 3 billion with an increase in the tax agency's cash collection of more than 5% in the January-August period. Therefore, the Government does not see a slowdown in economic activity; I want to move it publicly as there are other estimates, all of which are highly respectable but none comparable to the quality that gives the knowledge of economic activity through tax collection. I repeat, ladies and gentlemen, there is no economic slowdown in this second half of 2015, but quite the contrary, because the figures I am revealing today from the Chamber of Deputies do not even capture the whole of the summer; I am talking about the beginning of the summer, the split payments of both professionals and small and medium-sized enterprises, of economic activity in general, but what has been a summer that we all have to rate as very positive in terms of economic activity we are not yet reflecting in terms of tax collection. This, in short, insists on the argument that economic activity is strong in Spain, that domestic demand is growing at a good rate in our country. This is the best thing that can happen to us, and it explains that Spain is growing three times as much as the average of the euro countries because that makes it possible for us to have that growth and that job creation. By the way - it is tomorrow that the Minister of Employment and Social Security and the Vice-President of the Government have already insisted on this - we are leading the creation of employment in the developed world and that has to do with the strength of our economic growth, the characteristics of our economic growth, and fundamentally with the economic policy that the Government is doing during 2015, which is giving more strength to domestic demand, more capacity to invest, more capacity to consume all the economic agents of our country, because, I insist, the decline of the IRPF is a reality on the payrolls of the Spanish workers and, of course, of the officials and pensioners, who are subject to IRPF; its purchasing capacity is increasing, which is allowing us to have more growth and we are collecting more VAT, VAT from not only large companies, but from the expansion of tax bases of small and medium-sized enterprises. ParlaMint-ES_2015-09-15-CD150915.u61	Madam President, ladies and gentlemen, these are the second budgets that I have brought to this House - as I had the opportunity to do last year - and I do so with the satisfaction of being able to present a budget forecast that for justice policy will grow again in the forecast for 2016. Section 13, Justice For the purposes of budget submission, I would focus on three main elements. First of all, as I said to you, this is the second year that the budget of the Ministry of Justice, of the function of justice, is growing again, and it also does so in a relevant way. If the budget is approved in the terms presented by the Government, we will go to 7.24% increase. Therefore, we will be receiving a very significant increase in our 2016 allocations; 106 million euros for justice and, therefore, in absolute terms, is a very significant increase. Secondly, not only how much, that 106 million euros, that 7.24% increase, but where; what are we going to do with that increase? They are the most important sections, because there will be no increase in current expenditure, on the contrary, there will be a decrease of 1.7% thanks to savings in operating costs or in leases and transport, and there will be an increase, for example, in investments; nothing less than 26% which will be allocated in its entirety to technological modernisation and the improvement of judicial infrastructures. Therefore, it is not that the budget grows, it is that, moreover, it grows where justice needs it most. Then I'll stop for something else in these figures. Thirdly, the third of the ideas also has to do with a very relevant qualitative aspect, which is the social function, the role which with this budget will be strengthened by increasing by 5.9% the resources allocated to areas of special sensitivity to the citizen, such as free legal aid or support for victims of crime. Therefore, more resources for justice, more resources where they need to be, and more resources to bring justice closer to those who need it most. All this, of course, in the context of the budgetary lines marked by the Government for the year 2016, which have to do with supporting the growth of the Spanish economy and the creation of jobs; returning to public employees part of the effort they have made during these years; maintaining the welfare state by increasing social spending; and, finally, enhancing the good results of structural reform policies undertaken during this legislature and in which, in the case of justice, there has also been an important package of reforms that are supported through financial measures. From these bases, shared with the entire Government, we will ensure that the budgets for 2016 reinforce the pillars of rapid justice, an agile and efficient justice that guarantees the rights and freedoms of citizens; a justice that is solidarity and committed to the social value, which is always close to the victims, with whom they need specialized care, with whom they do not have sufficient resources and therefore deserve special protection; a digital justice, a justice without paper; a transparent justice, with more and better human resources, with more and better material resources and with more and better technological resources. That is the meaning and for what of the nearly EUR 1,578 million foreseen in the Ministry of Justice's 2016 expenditure budget. Not only does that figure increase by 7.24% compared to 2015, but if we add up to the resources that, since section 31, are incorporated into the Ministry of Justice as a result of the salary improvements of our staff, it is almost EUR 52 million as a result of salary improvements, we would be talking about a final allocation of almost EUR 1,630 million, which makes it clear - as I say - that we are the budget section that is going to grow the most in 2016 among all the ministries with competence in essential services. Entering into its distribution by chapters in a very succinct way, the staff expenditure chapter grows by 85 million euros, almost 7 %, to reach 1,303 million euros. As I was telling you, a justice with more human resources, for which 100 new judges and prosecutors will be incorporated and the job offer for 2016 will again incorporate, as it has already done in 2015, more than 2,000 places for the different bodies of the Administration of Justice. We will therefore strengthen our courts, courts and prosecutions with trained and qualified personnel. The chapter on current transfers will also grow by 7% -EUR 3.3 million in absolute terms - and therefore we will allocate EUR 56 million to key social and justice management policies, because we will increase grants for free legal aid and we will also increase resources for agreements with psychologists' colleges, which will allow us to improve and increase assistance to victims of crime and the realization of psychological expertise in judicial proceedings. Finally, a new appropriation of EUR 6 million is included in the 2016 draft budgets to, as we are doing this year, implement partnership agreements with the Autonomous Communities to work in coordination with the improvement of justice, the incorporation of new technologies and the provision of better material resources. To date we have already signed two agreements with the Autonomous Communities of Andalusia, Catalonia, Valencia, Cantabria or La Rioja, so we are already transferring resources for the Autonomous Communities to develop these types of projects. As I have told you, investment is also growing very significantly, almost 26%, slightly more in absolute terms of EUR 20 million than in the previous year. We will therefore go to a budget of EUR 98 million for investment expenditure which is basically aimed at two objectives: on the one hand, technological modernisation and, on the other, the improvement of infrastructure. With regard to technological modernisation, we will devote our efforts to improving registers, thanks to the 11 million investments planned for this, and to accelerating the intensification of justice in digital environments. Ladies and gentlemen, it is clear that in this area there is no other way than to advance in digital justice, network justice, paperless justice and innovative justice. With €65 million in budget earmarked for this item, I am sure that we will be able to make significant progress during 2016. The second of the investment lines has to do with the judicial headquarters, with the Infrastructure and Equipment Plan. In 2016, loans will grow again by EUR 19.4 million, an additional 4%, therefore, all the investment actions that are being carried out will either continue or increase. New construction works in judicial buildings, such as Badajoz, Guadalajara, Albacete, Segovia, Valladolid and Lorca; and rehabilitation and improvement works in Palencia, Ceuta, Soria or Trujillo. Likewise, we will continue with the financing of the construction and endowment of the justice building in Logroño, La Rioja, and for this will be allocated 3.7 million euros. I would also like to highlight a qualitative but very unique aspect, and therefore very important, that has to do with the renovation and modernization of the technological equipment of the National Institute of Toxicology, one of those very qualitative and unique institutions within our Administration of Justice, which will allow you to have state-of-the-art technologically advanced material: new electronic scanning microscopes with microanalysis systems for the service of forensics, new gas chromatographs and mass spectrometers for the service of chemicals and drugs, key in the analysis of drug or alcohol wickets, or new DNA sequerators, based on the identification of deceased, disappearances, paternity or pederastia investigations. I am now finishing, very succinctly, with an analysis of the budgets of the agencies attached to the department. To this end, the Centre for Legal Studies, where the training of prosecutors, secretaries, forensics and all officials of the Administration of Justice is carried out, will increase its budget by 18% in order to intensify its initial training and continuous training. The General Judicial Mutuality will have 101 million euros, slightly more than 2% to continue its assistance and performance work to all its mutualists. And the Data Protection Agency, which, as you are well aware, has its own legal personality and full independence, will have almost EUR 14 million to carry out its functions under the Data Protection Act. Therefore, the sum of agencies assigned plus the 55 million euros with which section 8 gives to the General Council of the Judiciary, leads us to a total aggregate for the different units of the function of justice of 1,746 million euros, which means in total 111 million euros more than the previous year and a close increase, the aggregate, to 7 %. Ladies and gentlemen, I would like to finish my speech by pointing out that this budget is certainly a material element, a financial element that helps us to do our job better, but that work is certainly done thanks to the service of all justice professionals: judges, prosecutors, secretaries, civil servants, all legal professionals - lawyers, prosecutors, social graduates - more than 226,000 workers who have allowed us to get here, to achieve a state of reform and to intensify the quality of our justice, in which there is certainly much to be done. I therefore trust that the 2016 budgets will bring us closer to that goal, to a goal of continuous and permanent reform of our administration of justice, a justice that will have sufficient financial resources to address all the challenges ahead. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

Thirdly, there are €75 million in energy efficiency, which has already been provided for the 2015 Efficiency Plan. And finally, there is a significant decrease in the costs of the electricity system of EUR 323 million. All this is what makes this decrease in the ministry's non-financial budget by 14.8%. As far as the financial budget is concerned, it amounts to EUR 2,379, a decrease of 0,36 % - and therefore practically the same - and is mainly due to the decrease in the national land observation programme in order to comply with the schedule of payments, included in the agreement signed between the Ministry of Industry, Energy and Tourism and the company Hisdesat for the financing of satellite known as PAZ. As far as the State Energy Secretariat is concerned, the budget amounts to EUR 4,226 million, which represents a decrease of 12.45 % and is mainly due to what I mentioned earlier: falling in the costs of the electricity system, falling in the allocations through the Institute for Diversification and Energy Saving and, also, to the energy efficiency programme which, I insist, has already been budgeted for 2015. As regards the Secretariat of State for Telecommunications and the Information Society, the budget amounts to EUR 797 million, which represents a 25 % drop, mainly due to the Digital Dividend Renewal Plan, which was successfully carried out during 2015. It is an expenditure that is already made, so we do not have to budget it for 2016 and therefore do not appear 273 million euros because they have already been spent this year. That's the reason for the decrease. With regard to this budget, we should highlight the development of the new General Telecommunications Act, which was adopted by a broad consensus in both Houses, approximately 95%, and I would like to thank the various parliamentary groups for their support. There is a new regulatory framework, new networks, new services, more investments. The fourth generation of mobile telephony has been deployed and, therefore, the new telecommunications framework is also being an engine of the economy. With regard to tourism, the total budget is EUR 313 million, which represents a slight increase of approximately 3%; in particular, 2.91 %. It must be borne in mind that this budget consolidates Spain's position. Thus, in terms of the tourist position in the world, we are the third country in the world in terms of the number of international visitors. Last year 65 million tourists visited us, and at the pace that the evolution of tourism is going this year, taking into account the good summer there has been, but also taking into account the winter season, we will probably be around 68 million tourists and therefore third in international tourism. I want to remember that in 2011 we were not the third but the fourth, but we are the second in terms of the total expenditure that these tourists make: the tourists who come to Spain spent last year 63 billion euros. In addition, and in relation to our competitors, we are the ones who have the most expenses per stay and per traveler. For an idea to be made, for an average stay in Spain the tourist spends approximately 880 euros, an amount that in France is 600 euros and approximately 700 euros in Italy. Therefore, third parties in visitors, second in expenses, but we have the number one place in the world ranking in terms of tourism competitiveness. What was the position we had four years ago in that global tourism competitiveness ranch? It wasn't number 1 but number 8. In 2013 we went from 8 to 4, and in 2015 for the first time Spain, touristically speaking, appears as the most competitive country in the world, and this not only has to do with the climate, the beaches, etc. - this is an indicator that takes place every two years by the World Economic Forum, and these are factors that are there, they are model data - but it has a lot to do with the importance that all public policies give to tourism policy, as well as the degree of digitization of the economy and what it can mean for digital tourism to incorporate digital development in Spain. It also has to do with the increase of sectors other than conventional ones, such as gastronomy, tourism of history, culture, architecture, shopping tourism, rural tourism, health tourism, and it has to do with security. Spain is one of the safest countries in the world, not only from the point of view of the tranquility in the streets of any city in Spain, but from the point of view that gives it the security of the National Health System managed by the autonomous communities. Therefore, this is a budget that follows a slight upward trend and will also serve to consolidate Spain's position as a tourist power in the world. I'll finally talk about the industry. Obviously the tourism sector is important, but we can not only live from tourism. In fact, with a few extraordinary years in the field of tourism in Spain and despite the fact that we are decreasing our unemployment rate, there is still 22% unemployment, so more industry is needed. As you know, since the beginning of the two-thousands of years the industrial sector had been losing weight within the gross domestic product, and the first time that there has been an inflection in this trend in the last fifteen years is in 2014, something that is not random. Behind there is a policy of macroeconomic stability without which industry can hardly advance, a policy of fiscal consolidation, a policy of structural reforms - there is labour reform, the reform of the financial system, the reform of the energy system - but there has also been an active industrial policy. Fortunately, many years ago in Spain, it was said that the best industrial policy was the one that did not exist. It is not true, the best industrial policy is not only the one that exists, but it also has to be an active industrial policy. That is what the Government has done since the beginning of this parliamentary term. How? First, different tools have been promoted and non-conventional financing tools promoted and strengthened. From the National Innovation Company, competitiveness plans and Reindus plans have been supported by specific industrial sectors, sectors that have a major tractor effect on the rest of the economy, such as automotive. This same morning, data were provided by the European Union on the evolution of car sales in the period January to August across Europe, and it turns out that in the face of growth in Europe between 6 and 7 %, Spain is growing to 22 % in that volume of sales. Behind is an active automotive policy that has made this sector again lead in terms of production, in terms of manufacturing, in terms of sales on the domestic market and also in terms of export. For this reason, the budget of the General Secretariat for Industry and Small and Medium-sized Enterprises is EUR 1,868 million, almost the same as this year, there is a slight increase of 0.26%. Finally, Mr President, I conclude by saying that these budgets consolidate the economic recovery in Spain, which is a clear and sustainable recovery. It is clear, because it is clear that where there was a recession four years ago, today the Spanish economy is growing, but it is not only growing but it is doing so twice the average rate that the rest of the euro area is growing. Moreover, there is recovery also because where there was during the four years of the last socialist legislature - years 2008 to 2011 - a destruction of 2,200 jobs per day - 3,400,000 jobs in the four years -, according to the data of the Labour Force Survey and the data of the unemployment recorded over the last twelve months, the process in which we are in is just the opposite. Employment is being created at approximately half a million jobs per year. If this economic policy in general and in particular industrial, tourism, energy and telecommunications policy persists, among other things, we are sure that in 2019 there will be 20 million people working again in Spain. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

They are not concerned about the strengthening of industry in those territories most in need of funding and public support, and do not solve the serious problem of unemployment and depopulation in the most disadvantaged areas. Thus, they have strategically abandoned sectoral and territorial policies. Moreover, they are even considering ending the annual public calls, as announced by the Secretary-General in her appearance; a fact that would impose discretion and further affect the lack of transparency of the ministry. We propose amendments with which we recover reindustrialization policies in disadvantaged areas affected by relocation processes, programmes to support industrial competitiveness in strategic sectors, as well as industrial observatories. We are creating a new heading for the financing of strategic industrial development projects and a greater allocation for the promotion of industrial innovation. Ladies and gentlemen, if in any way the management of your ministry has been highlighted in the chaotic energy legislation recently questioned by the European Commission. They have established a new electricity pricing system that places all its weight on the fixed term, which clearly discourages energy savings and penalises the most vulnerable consumers again. They have cut and attacked an incipient and innovative industry such as renewables and have penalized the development of self-consumption. Thus, the Government's energy effort will have been greater, but the results have not been more satisfactory. I would also like to highlight the amendment to update the General Radioactive Waste Plan to ensure the responsible and safe management of spent nuclear fuel and radioactive waste. On the other hand, we propose that the Elcogas integrated gasification technology plant, located in Puertollano, should benefit from the specific remuneration regime provided for in the Electricity Sector Act as a measure of support for a technology that, due to its innovative character and lack of maturity, cannot compete on the market on an equal basis with the conventional ones. With regard to the tourism sector, our amendments are aimed at promoting the development of Spain's competitive advantages as a sustainable tourist destination, with a strong sector and consolidating an international leadership position. We want the budgets to reflect a greater commitment to the sector, because their importance to our economy is undeniable - a commitment that does not reflect the Government with its cuts - and we highlight the amendments to build new hangers. Minister, I would like to take advantage of your presence - if I may - to convey to you the concern of the tourism sector in view of the negative consequences that the latest events that are taking place in the field of social tourism through the Imserso. For example, in the Valencian Community, thousands of jobs can be lost. Therefore, act immediately to avoid it and to put an end to the uncertainty under which the sector is immersed. Finally, in the area of promoting telecommunications and the information society, we propose greater allocations to ensure citizens' access to ICT, as a guarantee of equal opportunities, facilitating user training, increased financial support for ICT entrepreneurs and enterprises and SMEs and sufficient funding for local authorities. In short, we are proposing an important set of amendments to set in motion the engine of permanent growth of the economy, an engine totally neglected by this Government. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2015-09-15-CD150915.u91	Thank you, Madam President. Following the path taken by the minister, I will try to be as brief as possible, although I do not know if I will get that speed but I will try. These budgets which, as has been said throughout the day, are those of economic recovery, are budgets which speak to the clear that economic policy decisions give us greater or lesser capacity to decide on our future and therefore give us greater or lesser independence. These economic policies are very different from those experienced until 2011, policies of inaction that have led us to have to approve five budgets in a single legislature. With the correction of imbalances and the structural reforms undertaken we are in the situation in which we are today, which has perfectly defined the Minister before. With regard to energy, we have achieved the sustainability of the Spanish energy system. We came from a deficit of 26,000 million euros, 10 billion euros a year were accumulating gradually and between 2003 and 2012 there was an increase in the price of electricity bill of 82,8%. Once the reforms approved in this House were undertaken, reforms related to the Spanish electricity system, in 2014 there was a decrease in the average consumer tariff of approximately 5.8%. This has affected 27 million users, accounting for 60% of all consumers. All this has been possible with the reform of the electricity system and the change of methodology that was undertaken, as well as by facilitating more marketers and the change of supplier. These reforms of the system also affect coal. I have never heard the Socialist Party talk about coal, which was so much talked about throughout the legislature. It seems important to me to point out that this is the government that has made coal production centres viable so that they can be sustainable from the year 2018. This government has firmly committed itself to energy efficiency, which will undoubtedly be the fundamental pillar of energy efficiency in the next parliamentary term, providing the efficiency fund with more than 200 million euros. This will reduce not only emissions, but also the level of energy consumption we have in our country. This is the Government that has approved the interconnections that were paralysed and that allow to give greater guarantee to our electrical system and the incorporation of more renewable energy. I have heard different speakers talk about the crisis and the bankruptcy of renewable energy in our country. With the legislation that this government has implemented, we have guaranteed investments with an average return of more than 7 % and I have found a moment ago that renewable energy production was more than 40 % of the total energy produced in it. As far as industry is concerned, it has, as the Minister said earlier, been committed to full coordination with the private sector, which brought with it the adoption of the agenda last year. This budget shows a deep investment of more than 80% in financing support models and programmes. We want small and medium-sized enterprises to find more effective financing models and thus gain access to the credit they had renounced throughout the crisis period. Sectors that have a driving force in the Spanish economy have also been supported and promoted; I am thinking, for example, of the aerospace sector. This is the government that paid the outstanding debt to the European Space Agency. This Government, with that payment and support for the space agency, has achieved an increase in the presence of that industry in our economy and, with that tractor effect, that such investments attract contracts to high-value-added companies. Nothing to say about the car. Nor have I heard the opposition talk about the car situation. By 2011 car production had fallen by 40% in our country, and the figures are clear: we are already Europe's second-largest producer country and the ninth-largest country in the world, with more than 2.4 million units. With regard to tourism, I would like the opposition to begin memorizing these three fundamental steps to realize the weight that tourism has in our economy and that which is slowly gaining. In 2011 we did not have the volume of arrivals we have, which will far exceed 65 million, and to this we must of course add the increase in domestic demand, the position we occupy in competitiveness in the number of tourists entering and, fundamentally, the income per tourist. Let us remember that in the previous parliamentary term a greater budget in the field of tourism brought with it less investment and less tourists, an issue that has essentially to do with the policies that apply. We believe that the policies that the left is pushing in our country are an absolute mistake, mainly supported by the Socialist Party. They are aimed at tourist moratoriums, to prevent the arrival of cruises or to the establishment of tourist fees. All this succeeds in favouring the restrictions that limit tourism in our country. The Government is working with the Imserso and the Ministry of Development on bonuses. It is a coordinated work because the area of tourism belongs not only to the Ministry of Industry but also to the entire Government. In short, and ending with telecommunications, where we are going to move forward with the implementation of the digital agenda, this government has clearly faced in this legislature the need to develop our economy linked to sectors where employment is of the highest quality. We will therefore support, as it could not otherwise be, this budget. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

Ladies and gentlemen, I am here to present the budgets of the Ministry of Development for the next financial year 2016, since I believe that infrastructure, transport and housing are the three fundamental pillars for the modernization, territorial cohesion and the social and economic development of our country. For this reason, despite the economic crisis, the Government, through the Ministry of Development, has made an important effort with the budgets approved in the four years of the legislature, which is increased in this 2016 budget. We have put all the capacities of the department at the service of citizens and society, that is, at the service of cohesion, territorial vertebration and balance between all the autonomous communities and equal opportunities. In order to make further progress in this direction, today we are presenting budgets amounting to EUR 19,279 million, which means an increase of 10.2%. I would now like to turn to the practicality of a discussion of these characteristics, which is based on five major pillars or objectives. The first is nothing more than continuing to contribute to that economic development that I was talking about and to job creation. The second is to strengthen territorial cohesion and accessibility. The third is to improve the quality and efficiency of transport. The fourth, complete the reforms undertaken in this legislature. It will only be possible to achieve these objectives by focusing all of them on seven strategic measures or axes. The first is to continue implementing a realistic and efficient planning policy, based on the Pitvi, the logistics strategy and the proposals emanating from the advisory board. Ladies and gentlemen, we are now continuing with the housing sector, which is a priority sector for the Ministry of Development. We have adopted the new regulatory framework and are now moving forward with the change of model. The promotion of rent as a form of access to housing allows for greater flexibility, allows exiting the existing stock and bets on building rehabilitation, besides entailing huge energy savings, improving accessibility, generating employment and improving the quality of life of people. In the budgets of this legislature we have earmarked, including 2016, 3.560 million euros to promote access to housing and the improvement of the building. Ladies and gentlemen, aid under the State Plan 2013-2016 is benefiting more than 550,000 families throughout Spain: 230,000 social rental aid, 250,000 subsidy aid to families who are paying a mortgage and 72,500 aid for the rehabilitation of buildings. With regard to support for historical heritage, we have raised the traditional contribution from 1% of the budget to 1.5% of the culture. We have introduced mechanisms for advertising and competition in management. A total of 104 actions are under way, with a budget of over 62 million. Ladies and gentlemen, Madam President, the investment in housing in the budget amounts to 620 million. To continue with the State Plan for the Promotion of Rent, Building Rehabilitation and Regeneration and Urban Renewal, 533 million, of which 328 million will be allocated to State aid for rent and 205 million will be allocated to the maintenance of loan subsidies for protected housing buyers. On the other hand, there are specific agreements for the rehabilitation and remodeling of neighbourhoods: in Melilla, in Ceuta, in Lorca or in Las Chumberas, in Tenerife. In the case of rehabilitation and conservation of the heritage, we dedicate an investment of 30 million euros to continue with actions aimed at the conservation, restoration and rehabilitation of our rich historical-artistic heritage. This investment is complemented by the 1.5% cultural programme with EUR 47 million, which demonstrates our commitment and momentum in these actions. Thus, 62 actions for an amount of EUR 102 million are under way. In this way, ladies and gentlemen, considering both lines of action, we will have executed or launched 166 actions in this legislature in so many other buildings of unique historical and architectural heritage. I will give you some examples: the School of Music in Santo Domingo de la Calzada; the rehabilitation of the Royal Hospital of Guadix; the former convent of San Andrés, in Malaga; the castle of Vallmoll, in Tarragona; the rehabilitation of the walls of the Iberian city of Ullastret; the consolidation and recovery of the castle of Almansa; the rehabilitation of the cimborrio of the cathedral of Santiago, or the rehabilitation of the Campo del Príncipe building of the University of Granada or the intervention, as you know, of various milestones of the French road of Santiago. Madam President, ladies and gentlemen, lastly, I will point out to you the most significant aspects of the budgets envisaged for the Soil Corporate Public Entity, Sepes. To this day, ladies and gentlemen, even if it seems like a miracle, it has been achieved financial consolidation. You know: the result of that disproportionate purchase, the fall of industrial land that they said was residential, financed in many cases or in almost all with loans, we should have committed a plan of reorganization and enhancement of Sepes. Today, financial consolidation and consolidation of sales growth, 90 million euros, more than 12% have been achieved.
