ParlaMint-ES_2015-09-15-CD150915.u110	Thank you very much, Mr. Ayala. We shall now proceed to the debate on section 14. For the presentation of the section, I have the floor, on behalf of the Government, the Minister of Defence.
ParlaMint-ES_2015-09-15-CD150915.u111	Madam President, ladies and gentlemen, I come again to you to comment on the contents of section 14 of the draft General State Budgets for the coming financial year 2016. I thus compare, like many of my colleagues in the Government, for the fifth time in forty months, to continue to face the responsibilities that the Spaniards have entrusted to us, as we have done since the first minute of the legislature and as we will do until the last one in which it is appropriate to do the task. To a great extent this appearance of budgets has to do with responsibility. With the sole exception of the sending of Spanish soldiers and sailors to operations, no other task of our Administration requires taking on as much responsibility as the management of public money, those that the citizens put at our disposal, in order to guarantee their security and the defense of the sovereignty of Spain and of each and every one of the Spaniards wherever it is threatened. It is clear, even if it is not appropriate for some to admit, that the economic situation in the country has changed greatly and for good, as has been explained here repeatedly. That 22nd of May 2012, when it was my turn to defend my section for the first time, was a very complicated day, like most of them on those dates. We were working against clockwork to avoid the total wreckage of our economy and to restore credibility as a nation of Spain. We seemed to be engaged in failure given the poor state of public finances and the economy in general, almost collapsed after years of bad government. However, thanks to the responsible effort of the Spaniards and the responsible and courageously reformist management of the government led by Mariano Rajoy, today the reality is quite different, as has already been explained here too. Even without being able to neglect for a minute the fight against the public deficit, while intensifying efforts against unemployment that is still unacceptable, the crisis has been contained. We are growing again and we are doing so at the fastest pace among the big countries of the European Union. We are constantly creating jobs as the deficit diminishes and exports increase. In short, economic recovery is being consolidated. In line with the stabilization of public accounts, for the second year in a row and with compulsory moderation, we continue the growth path of the Ministry of Defence budget even from the modesty of 0.35%. For a department that has lost 32% of its budget in the last eight years, the containment of the fall is vital and any small increase makes us look to the future with hope and optimism, not only because we have reversed the trend but also because, as I told you last year, the really important thing is perseverance in all policies and particularly in state policies. That's what we're on. In this case, persevere Section 14, Defense has also been to end the bleeding and tend to gradually increase the defence budget. These are decisions that keep us on the road to an ambitious goal set by the Atlantic Alliance at the Newport Summit in Cardiff. Unfortunately, we still do not have enough budgetary space to consolidate our investment in defence as we would like and as Spain and its allies pledged to have it there, but we are moving forward for responsibility and coherence with ourselves and our allies. Although modest, this budget that I am presenting today is totally useful as a door to a path of confluence and stability in this policy consistent with the existence of the rule of law. From the very first moment of this parliamentary term, certainly exceptional in economic terms, from the first speech the Government and the parliamentary group that supports it have been insisting on the need for a great political pact that guarantees the sustainability and the capacity for modernization of national defence. We have applied this will to all important matters of the legislature. In some matters of great importance we have achieved this, in others we have not. The innumerable problems and shortcomings that we have had to face have not made it possible for the necessary conditions to be laid down in order to be able to raise an omni-understanding agreement realistically. This realism consists, first of all, of a criterion shared between all the political forces of the parliamentary arc or by a large majority: the investment in them. Investment in security and defence is irrefutable and will continue to be, given what has been and will continue to be this uncertain world. The budget allocation therefore needs to be stable and sustained over time. The second element for the agreement would be to start from a position where both the economic situation and fiscal consolidation would enable the most certain long-term financial perspective to be provided. Of course, the financial perspective would have to run in parallel with the defence planning cycle, which, as you know, starts with the national security strategy and the defence directive. The third element, finally, would be to have a completed planning cycle within the defence structure. I am referring both to your administrative apparatus and to an optimised, efficient and effective force; a department with the investment commitments acquired and future under a perfectly detailed control; a defense prepared, in short, to face with realism and responsibility the risks and threats that Spain can have, with short, medium and also long-term vision. Well, I am pleased to see, both from the text of some of the amendments which you have tabled and from certain public statements within and outside this House, that the lightness towards national defence has been left behind and that there is awareness of its indispensable and therefore irrefutable condition. This consciousness also seems to exist in society in general, although it may be the result of the obvious perception of the threat transmitted to us by the shocking and unfortunate images that are inescapable these days and which unfortunately are only signs of the reality that haunts us. Therefore, we can say that the first condition is given for the agreement. With regard to the second, as we have seen and as it is easily deduced from the draft law that we are dealing with today, we are not yet in a position to work and pool forecasts that can be fully assumed in the current economic framework. Like all our partners in the Union, we are bounded by our capacity to spend, to borrow and to finance within the European budgetary schemes that are binding on the Member States. However also at this point you start to see the light at the end of the tunnel. The deficit target for 2016 is already fully embedded in budgetary normality and although the greatest possible balance is always desirable and, of course, the appropriate surplus, the consolidation path agreed with the European Union is being met. This development, together with the improvement of the rest of the macro-economic dimensions, will allow for an economic context throughout the next parliamentary term that makes it possible to put forward, with due rigour, a multiannual budgetary planning for national defence. On the third point, ladies and gentlemen, I am pleased to inform you that, as I told you in 2012, we have correctly interpreted this crisis as an opportunity and have used it to adjust, adapt and optimize the structures of the Ministry of Defence, of the strength and expenditure in defence policy in general and of investment in particular. It has been an intense work of legislature and long-term vision, the results of which are reflected in the budget that I am presenting to you today. The quantity has been extensively detailed by the senior officials of the department, so I will inform you about the qualitative aspects of the budget. We have armed forces that, despite the economic difficulties, possess a sufficient degree of enlistment and preparation to face the risks and threats that Spain will have in this complicated beginning and throughout the twenty-first century. The men and women who make up them are the main asset of the defence and therefore, in addition to regaining purchasing power, like the rest of the public employees, this budget also adds the impact of the measures that we continue to implement to improve all aspects of their personal and professional lives. In this way, there is room for improvement of education and training schemes; policies of assistance, support and reconciliation, vacations, permits and leave, equal opportunities for men and women in access to and development of the military career, prevention, punishment and protection of any conduct constituting sexual harassment or on grounds of sex; promotion of professional exits for the continuity of careers and, of course, social protection, support to the family environment and to the families of the deceased and injured in service. Funds are also planned to alleviate the effects of geographical mobility on the exercise of the profession in the family environment, although these allocations will undoubtedly have to be improved in future years, in line with the recommendations arising from the report of the Military Life Observatory and which the honourable Members are currently processing. Of course, the reforms have not remained there. The chapter devoted to current expenditure clearly reflects the changes in the structure of the ministry and of the force, with the reallocation of items between budgetary services as a result of the empowerment of the Defence Staff as a major operational core and the organizational secondment of the EMU. We have also given impetus to the new challenges with the strengthening of cyber defence and intelligence, also in the homogenization of technical assistance and satellite observation services and in the centralization of contracts, both in the central body and in the Ministry of Finance, among other amendments to Chapter 2 that I have mentioned to you. The chapters on transfers also bear the fruit of the management improvements and reorganizations of the autonomous bodies carried out throughout the legislature, with the Invied, the university defence centres being the protagonists, the fulfilment of our international obligations and, with regard to Chapter 7, INTA as a major agglutinator of R & D and advanced engineering work. With regard to Chapter 6, the department's investment management has undergone a Copernican turn that is nevertheless surprising for its simplicity and effectiveness. EMAD plans and the Directorate-General for Arms and Materials manages and controls as a procurement agency. The Secretary of State has duly informed them of the situation of the special arms programmes. Thanks to the management of this government they have greatly reduced their actual and potential cost by more than 7 billion, while, as it could not be less, commitments have been paid on time for a figure that is approaching 5 billion euros, if we take into account the bill passed last week in the Senate. They are aware of the position of this Minister and the Government, which has been repeatedly explained to them. The initial budget for 2016 re-includes only management costs, because they remain pending both to fulfil the exact obligations for that year and to certify the economic availability to assume them. We remain fully convinced that the Government has to meet its obligations to international consortia and national suppliers, and we remain fully convinced that the forced procedure by the current situation is endorsed by law, by the doctrine of the advisory bodies and by the jurisprudence of the Constitutional Court. At the same time, the adoption at the time of the departure through the law brings it into a lengthy parliamentary debate, so that not only is the expenditure not stolen from delegated sovereignty, but it is enhanced and underlined. The Government is committed to the defence, the Armed Forces and the need to equip them with the material they need. It therefore considers that a solid defence industry is a strategic resource to which Spain cannot renounce and therefore listens and responds to the operational requests of the Armed Forces. At the moment they demand that we equip them with a system of remotely piloted aircraft of strategic character that will be incorporated into the 2016 budget. At the same time, and through the use of the 464.B programme of the Ministry of Industry, Energy and Tourism, we will have €86 million to continue in the new investment cycle with the financing of the technological projects of the future 8x8 programmes and frigate F-110. We talk about the real knowledge economy of R & D+i in what is the start of the new industrial defence policy. We are also talking about investments in a sector that generates more than 20,000 jobs in our country, which is leading and exporting, which has very high-skilled jobs, good wages and job stability and which recognizes and applauds this Government's commitment to its support. We are talking, as I said, about training and a vital capacity for our country's sovereignty. And we speak, taking up the crux of the matter, that the operation of our Armed Forces or enlistment and its ability to deal with the risks and threats that exist for our country depend directly on our ability to provide them with the best and most modern resources. This consists of deterrence, the first indispensable condition of a defence policy based on military capability. Therefore, all the reforms undertaken in investment management and industrial policy are essential, as it is essential to continue to evolve the technology available for our industrial capabilities both inside and outside Spain. That is why we believe that the third element for long-term financial-economic planning, the existence of a ministry and an updated armed forces at all its extremes, is more than fulfilled. Finally, it must be borne in mind that a new cycle of defence planning is to be carried out in the next legislature, which will undoubtedly start from a strategic economic position and from very different means and resources, much better than the situation in 2012. The 2016 budget reflects in a timely and comprehensive manner all the reformist work of this government during the parliamentary term. We have faced very difficult times, with very few resources, but we have been able to improve the entire structure precisely around the great objective of this ministry, which is nothing but to achieve the optimal operation and enlistment of our Armed Forces. In my 2012 speech I told them that the expression of the previous legislature, doing more with less, was a mantra, a beautiful phrase without any action accompanying it. On the contrary, what this Government has done has been to manage scarcity and to undertake an ambitious reform programme. We have not done more with less, but we have done much, very much, with very little. I should like to thank all the men and women of our Armed Forces and all those who serve in the civilian management bodies of the Ministry of Defence for their efforts and dedication. Without their work, under really adverse conditions, and their self-denial, we would not have obtained in any way the expected and recognized fruits within and outside Spain. Thanks to this work, we have a national defence prepared to face the increasing risks and threats and, thanks to this work, we have a defence policy framed so that, in the time frame of the next legislature, we can face the challenge of political consensus for its support and modernization. For this we will continue to work without pause. Thank you very much, Madam President. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2015-09-15-CD150915.u133	Thank you, President. Before we begin, ladies and gentlemen, I apologize in advance for having to leave the House before this debate is over, but I must inevitably take a plane at 7:30 p.m. I have the honour to present to this House the draft budget of the Ministry of Agriculture, Food and Environment for the financial year 2016, a budget that responds to the commitment of the Government to the development and growth of our country and to the promotion of the well-being of all its citizens. It has been developed in the context of the consolidation of an economic cycle change, which allows us to strengthen all those policies that result in direct benefits for our farmers, our farmers, our fishermen and our environment, that is, for society as a whole, and will contribute to the continuation and consolidation of this growth path. First of all, I will refer to its large figures and continue with the planned allocations to cover various policies and programmes of the department. The consolidated budget of the Ministry of Agriculture, Food and Environment for 2016 amounts to 9,765.9 million euros. As regards data for the State subsector, the unconsolidated budget will be EUR 1,976.6 million, virtually the same as last year. For the Section 23, Agriculture, Food and Environment autonomous bodies and the State Meteorology Agency the forecast is 8.391.95 million. With regard to the broad lines of expenditure policy, agriculture, fisheries and food policy will have a budget of EUR 7.438.2 million. The infrastructure policy, which includes actions on water resources and the environment, will reach €2,098.7 million. Meteorology policy will have 122 million. I have already broken down the Ministry's broad lines and programmes of action in 2016. In terms of staff costs, the budget for the next financial year amounts to EUR 365.7 million. In connection with this chapter, I would like to take this opportunity to express my sincere appreciation and personal gratitude for the dedication and dedication of the nearly 10,000 people who are members of the Ministry of Agriculture, Food and the Environment. His extraordinary work has made it possible in these years to achieve great achievements for the sector and for the whole of Spanish society. The Directorate-General for Agriculture, Food and Environment Services programme, which includes current expenditure on goods and services of the Ministry, without considering autonomous bodies and the Spanish Meteorology Agency, will be reduced by 6.7%, to EUR 38.5 million. If we include expenditure under Chapter 2 of autonomous bodies and Aemet, the adjustment amounts to EUR 7 million less than in 2015, i.e. a total of EUR 363,8 million. This is the result of the continuous improvement of management efficiency that allows us to maximize the funds allocated to policies that benefit citizens, who are with whom we have made a commitment. With regard to agricultural insurance policy, I am pleased to announce that, for the first time in six years, there is an increase in the allocation for agricultural insurance under the programme for forecasting risks in agricultural production. This is an increase of 6.1%, i.e. EUR 12 million, with a total allocation of EUR 211 million. This will increase the recruitment and insurance of insurance options with increased coverage, thereby strengthening the protection of farmers and livestock farmers against a greater number of risks. In addition, for needs that may arise throughout the year in the event of disasters and contingencies in the agricultural sector, we have foreseen an allocation of EUR 4.3 million. As regards the budget of the General Secretariat for Agriculture and Food, the programme on competitiveness and quality of production and agricultural markets amounts to EUR 32 million, 29% more than in 2015. This programme highlights the new €5 million budget dedicated to promoting the modernisation of agricultural machinery and technology in order to promote sustainable and more environmentally efficient use of agricultural machinery and technology, thereby also improving the competitiveness of the sector, and another half million dedicated to supporting its technical management. The budget dedicated to the testing and testing of tractors and other agricultural machinery for approval has also been increased by EUR 1 million. Integrated management of animal feed has been enhanced with 731,000 euros and with 700,000 euros the system of wine, fruit and vegetable information and table olives. The competitiveness programme of the agri-food industry and food quality has a budget of EUR 25.3 million, 38.4 % more than in 2015. This improvement has made possible the increase of almost all the lines included in Chapter 6, real investments, such as that dedicated to support for differentiated quality and organic agriculture, with EUR 872,630, i.e. 229% more than in 2015; the contribution to promotion and support for internationalisation, with EUR 6.6 million, 95% more than in 2015, and support for national and international fairs, with EUR 3.1 million, 121 % more than in 2015. As regards the actions in Chapter 7, capital transfers, the contribution to the promotion of agricultural co-operativeism for the structuring of supply, a priority of this government since the beginning of the legislature, with 3.5 million euros, 40% more than in 2015, and to the subsidies of the High Training Plan for managers of agri-food enterprises, with 353,000 euros, 38% more. In the area of rural development and forestry policy, the first thing to highlight is that for the period 2014-2020, in addition to the autonomous rural development programmes, a national rural development programme has been drawn up. The management of this national programme is divided between the Directorate-General for Rural Development and Forestry Policy, which has EUR 32.5 million, and the Spanish Agricultural Guarantee Fund, which has EUR 12.36 million. On the other hand, the Ministry guarantees its share of participation in the rural development programmes of the Autonomous Communities, with a reserve of EUR 119.5 million to transfer to the Autonomous Communities for their respective rural development programmes. Another priority policy will be - as it has been over the years - the fight against forest fires and reforestation actions. Both lines have been increased, the first up to EUR 84 million, and the second up to EUR 20 million. Water resources management for irrigation has 68.86 million euros, 53.6% more than in 2015. It includes a new irrigated investment line with €48.2 million, 19.42 % higher than 2015. The programme for competitiveness and quality of agricultural health also increases its budget by 2.8%, to EUR 36 million. Expenditure has been prioritized to maintain and strengthen areas such as the registration of plant protection products, support for the opening of new markets and foreign trade, national programmes for the prevention and control of pests in plant health and state programmes for the eradication of animal diseases, with €4.28 million, €3.51 million, €6 million and €8 million respectively. The Food Information and Control Agency maintains its budget of 635,000 euros. In the financial year 2016, in addition to the powers performed so far, it will assume official control of designations of origin, protected geographical indications of supraautonomic scope granted through Law 6/2015 of 12 May. As regards the EAGGF, most of its budget corresponds to the revenue and expenditure of the common agricultural policy. In the budget of this autonomous body, expenditure on direct aid to farmers and farmers and market regulation measures under Feaga will be EUR 5,747.38 million; this is 2.7% higher than the year 2015 in the funds of the common agricultural policy. The EAFRD rural development budget amounts to €866.9 million. In addition, under the other State social services programme, it is important to highlight EUR 107 million, five more than in 2015, from the European Fund for the Most Disadvantaged to implement the food aid programme for the most deprived. In 2016, national aid to the school milk programme, which will have 5.2 million euros, will also be launched. I now turn to the budgets for fisheries policy amounting to EUR 65.5 million. This allocation for the Spanish fishing sector will make it possible to optimise the fishing opportunities of our fleet and ensure the satisfactory exercise of this important economic activity. Not only did we consolidate last year's increase, but its budget was again increased by 1.57%. The financing of our fisheries policy is reinforced by three fundamental pillars for the proper and better management of fisheries resources for the benefit not only of today's but also of tomorrow's fishermen: searching for the best available scientific data, achieving maximum sustainable yield and monitoring compliance with existing regulations. The budget takes account of the forthcoming approval of the operational programme for the Spanish fisheries sector, which will make it possible to make full use of the new European Maritime and Fisheries Fund. In order to improve fisheries structures and markets, EUR 48.8 million will be allocated to finance, in agreement with the autonomous communities, fleet adjustment activities such as temporary and permanent cessations of fishing activity and the uniform application of these measures throughout the national territory. Following the entry into force of the new Common Market Organisation for the fisheries and aquaculture sector, a budget of EUR 4 million is envisaged to finance sectoral aid lines for the production and marketing plans of fisheries producer organisations and their associations and to improve the sales chain of their products. The area of control and inspection is strengthened, with an increase of 2.72%, highlighting the fight against illegal, unreported and unregulated fishing from which we have spent the last three years being leaders in the European Union, and this has become evident following the conduct of numerous control operations, although I would like to highlight, as inspection and control of important international impacts, operations Sparrow 1 and Sparrow 2. The protection of fisheries resources and sustainable development is provided with EUR 16.7 million. Scientific research, which will have a budget of EUR 11 million, will be enhanced; this will enable further fishing and oceanographic campaigns to obtain the best scientific advice. The aquaculture will have 1.87 million, compared to the 167,000 euros of 2015 and the national plans of marine crops and the development of the strategic plan of the Spanish aquaculture to promote and diversify the aquaculture activity will be implemented with these funds. In addition, in accordance with the landing obligation laid down in the new common fisheries policy, funds are allocated for studies to minimise the effects of the ban on discards. Its purpose is the development of an economically, socially and environmentally sustainable fishing activity. Finally, I should like to refer to the budgets for environmental policies. The total consolidated budget of the Secretariat of State for the Environment for 2016, including autonomous bodies and the State Meteorology Agency, amounts to EUR 1,370.4 million. To these must be added the 483.9 million that will invest the state water societies. Water policy is a priority for this Government. In this legislature we have updated hydrological planning and have committed to a balanced and supportive management of water. We are also working to ensure the provision of an essential public service by fulfilling the obligations of the European Union. Overall, the Directorate-General for Water, water confederations and state-owned companies will invest more than EUR 1,252 million. 589 million will be allocated to the water infrastructure management programme to implement the measures and actions envisaged in the hydrological plans with the aim of ensuring supply, as well as to respond to extreme weather events, such as floods or droughts. With a budget of EUR 179.3 million, the water quality programme will make it possible to continue to make orderly progress in the area of purification and sanitation, i.e. to improve the quality of our waters, the water of our rivers and thereby improve our environment. The €483.9 million invested by state water companies will be earmarked for supply, sanitation and irrigation. For the sustainability of the coast and the sea, the action programme on the coast will have an investment of 64.78 million euros. This appropriation will be intended to control coastal regression, protect and recover coastal systems, ensure the integrity of the maritime-terrestrial public domain, maintain the good environmental status of the marine environment and restore accessibility to the coast in order to ensure its use and enjoyment by citizens. In addition, coastal protection strategies will be put in place and we will continue to implement the coastal adaptation strategy to the effects of climate change. The environmental protection and improvement programme will have EUR 12 million and will help to improve, speed up and streamline environmental assessment procedures. It will also allow for the development of air quality assessment actions or related to noise pollution, the application of aspects of existing waste legislation or the development of the future State Waste Management Framework Plan. Under the protection and improvement of the natural environment programme, EUR 16.8 million will be allocated to actions to protect biodiversity, restore and recover degraded ecosystems, reduce or eliminate threats to native biodiversity or prevent and combat invasive alien species. The action programme for pollution prevention and climate change will have a budget of EUR 49 million. A new budget of 18 million will be made available for the implementation of new environmental stimulus plans, and EUR 17 million will be allocated to PIMA Adapta for actions to adapt to climate change on our coasts, in the area of water and biodiversity, 42% more than in 2015, i.e. EUR 5 million more. The carbon fund will provide €9.18 million to meet a new and extensive call for climate projects. This consolidates our climate change policy. We dedicate EUR 45 million to effectively reducing greenhouse gas emissions in our country; EUR 45 million to change our economic model to a low carbon model and we do not want to buy emission allowances from other countries, as I say, by encouraging the low carbon economy in Spain, by encouraging the reduction of greenhouse gases in Spain, so that we can also make an environmental policy that contributes to the generation of employment. The Autonomous National Parks Agency will have 21.7 million euros destined mainly for the promotion and strengthening of the functions of general coordination. Through common and horizontal programmes, the network of parks, strengthened after the adoption of Law 30/2014 on National Parks, will be vertebrate and cohesive. The 60.2 million euros of the State Meteorological Agency will ensure the adequacy of the infrastructures for meteorological observation and predictions and provide for the payment of contributions to international meteorological bodies. The Biodiversity Foundation, with €7.62 million, will be able to undertake more than 30 initiatives in the development of its strategic lines of action. In short, the budget allocation for environmental actions increases the investment effort made by the Government, which has made the environment an ally of economic and social growth in a sustainable way. Ladies and gentlemen, the budget of the department for 2016 that I have just presented has the necessary resources to carry out the actions required by the Spanish agri-food and fisheries sector and the improvement of our environment, thus contributing to the further promotion of economic growth and employment generation in Spain. Thank you very much, Madam President. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2015-09-15-CD150915.u155	Thank you very much, President. Ladies and gentlemen, I have the honour to appear in this House to defend Section 27 of the General State Budgets for 2016. We are faced with budgets that reflect the full consolidation of the recovery of the Spanish economy. Just over three years ago, in May 2012, he was defending in this very House the first budgets of this legislature. As you will recall, our economy was then on the verge of collapse due to the loss of confidence and the deep recession in which it was plunged. This has been confirmed today by the revision of the national accounts published by the INE to which I will refer later. On the contrary, the situation today is completely different. The budgets you have are those of a country that leads growth within the main economies of the euro area and also the advanced ones. This is acknowledged by all the forecasts of international bodies such as the International Monetary Fund, the OECD and the European Commission. Public accounts for 2016 aim to continue and strengthen recovery. Therefore, they preserve the same principles that have made possible the important turn of the Spanish economy, are prudent and show the Government's firm commitment to the goal of the public deficit. Ladies and gentlemen, in my presentation I shall first address the current situation in our economy, Section 27, Economy and Competitiveness, starting with a reference to the international context. Secondly, I will briefly refer to the macroeconomic framework on which the budgets are based and then give concrete expression to the main items of my department focusing on trade and R & D+i, both fundamental pillars for sustained growth. As far as the international context is concerned, as you know, the main agencies predict moderate growth in the world economy for this year and next. To this is added the increase in the downward risks observed in recent months by a number of factors: firstly, the slowdown in China and the volatility of the markets observed this summer; secondly, the worse behaviour of emerging economies in the face of the fall in the price of raw materials; thirdly, the concern in these economies about the impact of a possible rise in interest rates in the United States and, finally, the deep slowdown in international trade. As far as the euro area is concerned, the recovery driven by the tailwinds we already know is expected to continue: the fall in oil prices, the depreciation of the euro and the measures launched by the European Central Bank. However, the pace at which progress is being made, the improvement in Europe, remains weak and is therefore a matter of concern. This context contrasts with the strength of the growth of the Spanish economy, mainly thanks to the important progress made in correcting the imbalances that have enabled the ambitious reforms undertaken. This has been clearly reflected in the latest data published by the INE for the second quarter of the year. The Spanish economy grew by 1% in the quarter, a rate that had not been recorded since before the crisis and which almost tripled the euro area average. This dynamism is also recorded in the labour market. As I explained in my appearance last July, the second-quarter EPA shows an increase in the number of people employed by 412,000. This is the largest quarterly increase in the last ten years. In addition, the advanced indicators known to date indicate that this improvement continues in the third quarter; those of confidence and economic sentiment continue to peak and the August MIPs reflect that the strength of the activity is maintained. The evolution of credit flows to the private sector is also revealing, as the latest available data showed. The credit to households and SMEs chain about twenty months in positive, confirming the change in the situation of our banking sector. In short, ladies and gentlemen, all the data available so far indicate that the good performance of the economy will continue in this third quarter, with annual growth of about 3.5%. I now turn to the macroeconomic scenario that accompanies the General Budgets of the State. Allow me not to over-stop as I had the opportunity to present it to you in my appearance last July. As you know, this is a realistic macroeconomic picture, based on prudent assumptions and which shows the recovery of the Spanish economy. We estimate GDP growth at 3.3% this year and 3% by 2016, in line with the consensus of analysts and projections of major international agencies. This activity is also based on a balanced pattern thanks to the strength of external demand and the improvement of the external sector. For its part, the labour market will continue to be strengthened by the creation of more than 600,000 jobs in EPA terms, as I have already mentioned to you on other occasions. This will result in the number of people employed at the end of this year being above the levels we are at the beginning of the parliamentary term. For its part, the unemployment rate, which is already below the fourth quarter of 2011, will continue its reduction and we expect it to reach 19.7% in 2016. This favourable development of growth and employment would not have been possible without the important correction of the imbalances achieved over the years. The correction of the external imbalance, the significant progress in the deleveraging of the private sector, the consolidation of the banking sector and the reduction of the public deficit have been key elements. Budgets for 2016 will continue this process of improving our economic foundations. Thus, the public deficit will fall below 3% of GDP next year and a primary surplus will be recorded for the first time since the start of the crisis. In addition, the public debt/GDP ratio will begin its downward path, decreasing for the first time since 2007. Improving our public accounts has been essential to restore confidence in the economy. This is clearly seen in the evolution of our financing costs. Thus, the average cost of issuing the Spanish Treasury marked last August as a historical minimum, 0.87%, which contrasts with almost 4% we paid in 2011. In addition, this has been compatible with an extension of the mid-life of the portfolio, another sign of improving the perception of the future of our economy. The share of public debt in the hands of non-residents is also 45% compared to 34% in 2011. Ladies and gentlemen, I shall now go over the main budget items to be implemented by the Ministry of Economy next year. I will begin by referring to the Secretary of State for Trade. Let me remind you of the important role that the foreign sector has played, is having and will have in the continuation of the recovery of the Spanish economy. Thus, 2015 will be the third consecutive year of current account surplus, which will also be around 2% of GDP, a figure that has not been given in our modern economic history. The macroeconomic picture predicts that current surpluses will continue for another three years, until 2018. We also expect seven years of financing capacity for the rest of the world, bearing in mind that this 2015 will be the fourth year in which we achieve it. This will allow us to continue to reduce our external debt and, therefore, to continue to correct our main imbalances. Within the good performance of the external sector, the dynamism of exports, which in the first half of the year grew to 5% every year, stands out. During this period, sales abroad exceeded 125 billion euros. This is the highest amount in a semester of our entire historical series and significantly higher than the European average. Thus, the budgets of the Secretary of State for Trade for 2016 are increased by 1.5% compared to this year, to almost 500 million euros. We have made special efforts to increase the allocations for internationalization, which for the third consecutive year are increasing. With regard to ICEX, it highlights the increase in transfers of EUR 1.2 million. In addition, within the process of rationalization and efficiency that has been carried out within the Administration, this entity has completed the process of integration of different agencies that we begin at the beginning of the legislature. I now turn briefly to domestic trade items, a sector of particular importance to the Spanish economy, which accounts for almost 12% of GDP. I would like to highlight the good development of retail trade, whose sales have increased by more than 4% in real terms in July compared to the same month of the previous year. Undoubtedly, behind this dynamism lies the recovery of domestic demand and the increase of consumer confidence. This is why these budgets maintain trade support and endowment through the Plan of Support for the Competitiveness of Retail Trade. But as important as budgetary efforts have been the legislative measures implemented in the area of retail trade in recent years. Among many others I would like to highlight the limit to interbank exchange rates in card payments, since Spain was the first country to incorporate it into its legislation. Ladies and gentlemen, I will finally refer to the budgets of the State Secretariat for R & D & i for 2016. Firstly, I would like to point out that the grant chapter has increased by EUR 227 million, which is 15% more than in the previous year, and is the third consecutive year of increases. Also for the third consecutive year, transfers to public research bodies, IPOs, increased by 20 million compared to the previous year, a situation that contrasts sharply with the decline of more than 200 million experienced in 2010. I would particularly like to highlight the situation of the Higher Council for Scientific Research, the third research body in Europe. Thanks to the extraordinary actions committed in 2013 today has recovered its normality and overcome its financial difficulties, increasing by more than 8.5 million euros in 2016 its budgets compared to the previous year. But, ladies and gentlemen, if there is a party in which the Government has demonstrated its commitment to science and technology, it has been with the research staff. We find ourselves with a replenishment rate that has been decreasing, in 2009 it was 30%, in 2010 it was 15% and in 2011 it was 10%. This Government, in a very complex economic situation, has managed to implement a replacement rate of 100 per cent already in this year and will also be 100 per cent next year. We will also have another 25 contracts for those researchers who are pursuing a non-functional career, bringing together 75 contracts of this type. It is a model based on merit, very widespread in Europe. I would also like to remind you that we have recovered the social security bonuses. We have turned predoctoral scholarships into contracts and created industrial doctorates to bring research closer to companies, one of our main challenges. As far as our quotas in international agencies are concerned, the Government remains committed to a model of large-scale facilities in the face of the dispersion of efforts. In 2016 we will reduce the debt to zero with the CERN, the particle accelerator located in Geneva, which carried a debt of 40 million euros since 2010. We have also committed to the European supercomputer initiative through the Barcelona Supercomputing Center to which we have allocated €7.5 million. Spain thus maintains its leading position in Europe in this area. With regard to CDTI, the main support for private research in our country, will have 30 million for direct aid in addition to credits. This will enable initiatives such as the new Neotec programme to finance new business projects in their early stages, or also a new seed capital initiative to boost venture capital interested in technology-based SMEs. If there is a game that has experienced a qualitative leap, it has been the national funds for research projects, one of the traditional demands of the scientific community. The National Fund for Scientific and Technical Research for the main calls for human resources and research projects increases by EUR 150 million, more than 43 %, and the Science, Technology and Enterprise Fund with which we provide calls for transfer and public and private cooperation is increased by EUR 20 million, 28 %. Ladies and gentlemen, before I conclude, I would like to refer to the establishment of the research agency whose statute will be approved by the Council of Ministers in the coming weeks. It will involve the integration of all instruments for financing scientific research and will give more stability to calls. It will also improve resource allocation and reduce bureaucracy. Its budget will be made available through the reallocation of funds managed by the State Secretariat for R & D & I. Ladies and gentlemen, I conclude. The broad budget lines for the Ministry of Economy and Competitiveness for the next financial year reflect the profound change in our economy. In fact, the revision of the national accounts published by the INE this morning has confirmed that the economic crisis occurred before what was thought and has been more serious, painful and intense than was estimated. Ladies and gentlemen, Spain did not experience any green outbursts and the recovery never took place. The economy did not grow to 0.7% in 2011, as we were told at the beginning of the legislature. Now we know it was down to 1%. This data further highlights the unmatched course change that we have given in just over two years. In 2012 we have a free-falling economy and a strong job destruction that generated huge doubts and mistrust and now we have the fastest growing economy in the euro area with strong job creation, moderate prices and external surpluses. In just three years we have been able to turn the longest and deepest crisis in our modern history, an unprecedented turn that has been possible thanks to the efforts made by Spanish society and the economic policy implemented. The data published today by the INE have shown that the loss of income from 2008 to mid-2013 was not 7 points, as originally estimated, but almost 9 times more than four times the GDP loss recorded by the euro area during the same period. All this does if it is more important to continue with the course taken. It is essential to maintain the pace of recovery at 3% rates in the coming years. If we do not, we will not be able to put the crisis behind us once and for all and the deep social gaps in the crisis will be open for a long time. We shouldn't be fooled. Reversing reforms and undoing the road ahead pose the main risks to the economy and all efforts made so far will have been in vain. On the contrary, if we persevere on the path undertaken, we will have a much better future before us. It is everyone's responsibility to work to achieve this. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

We also ask for a contribution from the Barcelona Metropolitan Transport Authority for the financing of regular passenger transport on account of the contract-programme which is signed with the State, in this case of 60 million, to balance the deficit of the system and avoid a high rate increase that would affect the whole of the citizenry. We therefore propose that the contribution of the State be equivalent to that made in previous years. Similarly, we propose allocations to finance investments in the northern part of Granada, fulfilling the political commitment of the Government of Spain to the local and Andalusian administrations for the implementation of an integral plan in one of the largest social marginalization exchanges in Andalusia. We also request an extraordinary contribution of EUR 22 million for the implementation of the Unit Act in Castile-La Mancha, bearing in mind that a recent court ruling has just overturned the order by which the Government of Cospedal produced a significant cut in the implementation of the Unit Act leaving thousands of potential beneficiaries and their families without the expected help to cover situations of extreme need. This ruling obliges thousands of files to be reviewed, with the consequent retroactive recognition of certain benefits, which would mean and would result in a further collapse of the system of dependency in the region. This extraordinary help will be important to replenish the rights of many dependents without creating new waiting lists. There is also an extraordinary contribution of 20 million to the University of Castile-La Mancha, which in recent years has suffered a significant fall in its public funding of close to 60%, which has affected not only the number of teachers, the continuity of research projects, etc., but has paralysed the construction of faculties such as that of medicine in the Royal City. There has also been a drop in the number of students due to the reform of the scholarship system, so an extraordinary contribution is needed to recover plans, teachers and students and for the University of Castilla-La Mancha to serve the economic, social and cultural development of the community. Finally, for the community of Catalonia, and in compliance with the investment regulations in its autonomy status, we request an additional €914 million. Compliance with the third additional provision of the Statute of Autonomy of Catalonia disappeared in the General Budgets of the State of 2012, in those of 2013 and also in those of 2014. From our point of view, this represents a very serious institutional disloyalty; therefore, we cannot fail to request a fairer allocation for Catalonia that equates the State's investment in infrastructure in Catalonia, excluding the Interterritorial Compensation Fund and the relative share of Catalonia's gross domestic product in relation to the State's gross domestic product. These would be some of the main amendments that our group has tabled in section 32. Thank you very much, Madam President.
